# API inventory

Generated by `tools/inventory` from type-checked source (`go/packages` and `go/types`), as spec 5.1 requires.
Do not edit by hand: regenerate with the command at the end of this section.

| Input | Directory | Commit | Vendor module version |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tool | tools/inventory | 7256491dd7f3bc97521e0cebe4e1af70c7fad4f2 |  |
| server tree | hoardDB | 11fca11e6e8f608e3f24c35588866e76476098af | v1.17.10 |
| driver tree | hoarddb-go | 5190afbe0257427fef66916e55b1f9130978851d | v1.17.10 |

Regenerate (the toolchain must be the server's `go` directive or newer, so the type checker reads its standard library):

    cd tools/inventory && GOTOOLCHAIN=go<server go version> go run . \
      -tool-commit <this tool's commit> -server <hoardDB dir> -server-commit <hash> \
      -driver <hoarddb-go dir> -driver-commit <hash> -o ../../docs/api-inventory.md

What the tool cannot see: a target whose static type is `interface{}`, a non-empty interface or a type parameter is
marked `pending tap` and completed by the runtime tap (spec 9.1.7, build step 6a). An error that reaches a print, compare
or branch only after being returned through other functions is not followed: section 5 lists the places where an error
variable that received a vendor call's error is used, plus every use of a vendor error type or sentinel. The tool does
not attempt per-site quirk notes (spec 5.1 item 3).

Which assignment reaches an error use is decided by source order and nesting, not by control flow. A use counts as a codec
error use when the nearest preceding assignment in a block that also holds the use, or any nearer assignment in a block that
does not, is a vendor codec call. So a reassignment on every branch of an `if` still leaves the use labelled (an extra row),
and an assignment that comes later in a loop body than the use is not followed (a missing row).

## 2. Whole method sets

A type is listed when a tree names it or any expression in the tree has it (or holds it in a slice, array, map, pointer
or result). It is mirrored whole: interface satisfaction is invisible to references (`fmt` calls `String`, `encoding/json`
calls `MarshalJSON`, the codec calls `IsZero`). `implicit` lists the methods of that kind.

struct; 0 references by name, reached only through expressions (no reference by name).

### String renderings

`explicit` is a call to a vendor `Hex`, `String` or `GoString`. `implicit fmt` is a vendor value with a `String` method passed to
`fmt` or `log`. `implicit json` is a vendor value reached by `encoding/json`, which renders it through `MarshalJSON`. `json tag` is a struct field
with a `json` tag that holds such a value: the struct reaches `encoding/json` wherever it is marshalled, even through an `interface{}`.

| implicit fmt | 12 |

## 6. Boundary functions

The enclosing function of every call to a configured boundary function (build step 7b). A function that appears for
several boundaries is one function. `Calls` counts the call expressions inside it. A boundary with no definition is listed
so that a rename is visible.

Distinct enclosing functions: 30 outside tests, 38 with tests.
